\displaystyle=\int_{f(t,x)>\epsilon}\ln^{+}(f(t,x))\,\psi^{2}(t,x)dx+\int_{f(t,x)\leqslant\epsilon}\ln^{+}(f(t,x))\,\psi^{2}(t,x)dx

\displaystyle\triangleq\textnormal{I}_{1}+\textnormal{I}_{2}.

To estimate
\textnormal{I}_{1}
we employ the fact that
x\mapsto\ln^{+}(x)
is a decreasing function in order to get

\textnormal{I}_{1}\leqslant(\ln^{+}\epsilon)\|\psi(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

(4.47)

To treat the term
\textnormal{I}_{2}
, Hölder and Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequalities yield

\displaystyle\textnormal{I}_{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\left(\int_{f(t,x)\leqslant\epsilon}\left(\ln^{+}(f(t,x))\right)^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\psi(t)\|_{L^{4}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\left(\int_{d(x,\Sigma_{0})\leqslant\epsilon}\left(\ln^{+}d(x,\Sigma_{0})\right)^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\psi(t)\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\psi(t)\|_{L^{2}},

where we have used that
{\Psi}
preserves the Lebesgue measure. On the other hand, we get from the inequality
\ln^{+}t\geqslant 1
, the assumption (
4.9
) and some
\sigma>0

\int_{d(x,\Sigma_{0})\leqslant\epsilon}\left(\ln^{+}d(x,\Sigma_{0})\right)^{2}dx\leqslant\int_{d(x,\Sigma_{0})\leqslant\epsilon}\left(\ln^{+}d(x,\Sigma_{0})\right)^{4}dx\lesssim\epsilon^{\sigma}.

Therefore, combining this estimate with Young inequality together with (
4.47
) imply

4A(t)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\ln^{+}\big(d(\Psi^{-1}(t,x),\Sigma_{0})\big)\psi^{2}(t,x)dx\leqslant\left(-4A(t)\ln\epsilon+CA^{2}(t)\epsilon^{\sigma}\kappa^{-1}\right)\|\psi(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\kappa}{2}\|\nabla\psi(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

and then, (
4.45
) yields by applying Gronwall inequality

\|\psi(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leqslant C_{0}^{2}(1+t)^{3}\kappa\epsilon^{-4\int_{0}^{t}A(\tau)d\tau}\exp\left(\epsilon^{\sigma}\kappa^{-1}C\int_{0}^{t}A^{2}(\tau)d\tau\right).

Hence, the choice
\epsilon=\kappa^{\frac{1}{\sigma}}
enables to write

\|\psi(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leqslant C_{0}^{2}(1+t)^{3}\kappa^{1-\frac{4}{\sigma}\int_{0}^{t}A(\tau)d\tau}\exp\left(C\int_{0}^{t}A^{2}(\tau)d\tau\right).

On the other hand, we infer from (
4.31
) and (
4.14
)

\int_{0}^{t}A(\tau)d\tau\leqslant\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{V}^{\prime}(\tau)e^{\mathbb{V}(\tau)}d\tau=e^{\mathbb{V}(t)}-1

(4.48)

which implies, since
\kappa\in(0,1)
,

\|\psi(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leqslant C_{0}^{2}(1+t)^{3}\kappa^{\Theta_{1}(t)}\exp\left(C\int_{0}^{t}A(\tau)^{2}d\tau\right),

with

\Theta_{1}(t)\triangleq 1+\frac{4}{\sigma}\Big(1-e^{\mathbb{V}(t)}\Big).

By setting

[0,T_{2}]\triangleq\Big\{t\in[0,T_{1}]:\Theta_{1}(t)\geqslant 0\Big\}

(4.49)

we deduce that for any
\kappa\in(0,1)

\forall\,t\in[0,T_{2}],\quad\|\psi(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leqslant C_{0}^{2}(1+t)^{3}\exp\left(C\int_{0}^{t}A^{2}(\tau)d\tau\right).

(4.50)

\bullet

L^{\infty}-
estimate
. Let
t\in[0,T_{2}]
, where
T_{2}
is given by (
4.49
) and decompose
\psi
, the solution of (
4.28
), as follows

\displaystyle\psi=\psi_{1}+\psi_{2}+\psi_{3},

(4.51)

with

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\big(\partial_{t}+{v}\cdot\nabla-\kappa\Delta\big)\psi_{1}(t,x)=\|v(t)\|_{L(\Sigma_{t})}\ln^{+}(d(x,\Sigma_{t}))\,{\bf{1}}_{f(t,x)>\epsilon}\psi(t,x),&\\
\psi|_{t=0}=0,\end{array}\right.

(4.52)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\big(\partial_{t}+{v}\cdot\nabla-\kappa\Delta\big)\psi_{2}(t,x)=\|v(t)\|_{L(\Sigma_{t})}\ln^{+}(d(x,\Sigma_{t}))\,{\bf{1}}_{f(t,x)\leqslant\epsilon}\psi(t,x)&\\
\psi|_{t=0}=0,\end{array}\right.

(4.53)

and

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\big(\partial_{t}+{v}\cdot\nabla-\kappa\Delta\big)\psi_{3}(t,x)=\kappa\Delta\bar{\varphi},&\\
\psi|_{t=0}=0,\end{array}\right.

(4.54)

where the function
f
is defined in (
4.46
). To handle (
4.52
), we combine the maximum principle with (
4.36
) and (
4.14
) allowing to get for any
\epsilon\in(0,e^{-1})

\|\psi_{1}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leqslant-\ln\epsilon\int_{0}^{t}A(\tau)\|\psi(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}}d\tau.

(4.55)

To estimate
\psi_{2}
, it is enough to make use of (
4.36
) and (
4.14
) and apply Lemma
A.2
, with
F=0
,
p_{1}=6
and
q_{1}=\frac{4}{3}

\displaystyle\|\psi_{2}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\big(1+(\kappa t)^{{\frac{1}{12}}}\big)\kappa^{-\frac{5}{6}}\left\|A\;\ln^{+}f\;\mathbf{1}_{\{f\leqslant\epsilon\}}\psi\right\|_{L^{6}_{t}L^{\frac{4}{3}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+t)^{{\frac{1}{12}}}\kappa^{-\frac{5}{6}}\|A\|_{L_{t}^{6}}\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}\left(\int_{f(\tau,x)\leqslant\epsilon}\left(\ln^{+}f(\tau,x))\right)^{4}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}\|\psi\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}},

Therefore we find by virtue of (
4.46
) combined with the fact that
{\Psi}
preserves Lebesgue measure together with (
4.9
) and (
4.50
)

\|\psi_{2}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leqslant C_{0}(1+t)^{\frac{3}{2}+{\frac{1}{12}}}\kappa^{-\frac{5}{6}}\epsilon^{\frac{\sigma}{4}}\|A\|_{L^{6}_{t}}\exp\left(C\int_{0}^{t}A^{2}(\tau)d\tau\right).

(4.56)

Let us now move to estimate
\psi_{3}
. Using once again Lemma
A.2
with
G=0
,
q=\infty
and
p=3
implies

\displaystyle\|\psi_{3}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leqslant C\big(1+(\kappa t)^{\frac{1}{6}}\big)\kappa^{\frac{1}{3}}\|\nabla\bar{\varphi}\|_{L^{3}_{t}L^{\infty}}.

Thanks to (
4.43
), it follows that for
\kappa\in(0,1)

\displaystyle\|\psi_{3}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{0}(1+t)^{\frac{4}{3}+\frac{1}{6}}\kappa^{\frac{1}{3}}.

(4.57)

To achieve the estimate of
\psi
in
L^{\infty}-
norm, we collect (
4.55
), (
4.56
) and (
4.57
) to obtain

\|\psi(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leqslant C_{0}(1+t)^{2}\left(\kappa^{\frac{1}{3}}+\kappa^{-\frac{5}{6}}\epsilon^{\frac{\sigma}{4}}\|A\|_{L^{6}_{t}}\exp\left(C\int_{0}^{t}A^{2}(\tau)d\tau\right)\right)-\ln\epsilon\int_{0}^{t}A(\tau)\|\psi(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}}d\tau.

Thus,
Gronwall lemma yields

\|\psi(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leqslant C_{0}(1+t)^{2}\left(\kappa^{\frac{1}{3}}+\kappa^{-\frac{5}{6}}\epsilon^{\frac{\sigma}{4}}\|A\|_{L^{6}_{t}}\exp\left(C\int_{0}^{t}A^{2}(\tau)d\tau\right)\right)\epsilon^{-\int_{0}^{t}A(\tau)d\tau}.

By making the choice
\epsilon=\kappa^{\frac{14}{3\sigma}}
, we end up with

\|\psi(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leqslant C_{0}(1+t)^{2}\left(1+\|A\|_{L^{6}_{t}}\exp\left(C\int_{0}^{t}A^{2}(\tau)d\tau\right)\right)\kappa^{\frac{1}{3}\big(1-\frac{14}{\sigma}\int_{0}^{t}A(\tau)d\tau\big)}.

Thereafter, by using (
4.48
), we find that

\|\psi(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leqslant C_{0}(1+t)^{2}\left(1+\|A\|_{L^{6}_{t}}\exp\left(C\int_{0}^{t}A^{2}(\tau)d\tau\right)\right)\kappa^{\frac{\Theta_{2}(t)}{3}},

with

\Theta_{2}(t)\triangleq 1+\frac{14}{\sigma}\Big(1-e^{\mathbb{V}(t)}\Big).

By setting

[0,T_{3}]\triangleq\Big\{t\in[0,T_{2}]:\;\Theta_{2}(t)\geqslant 0\Big\}.

(4.58)

we conclude that for
\kappa\in(0,1)

\forall t\in[0,T_{3}],\qquad\|\psi(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leqslant C_{0}(1+t)^{2}\left(1+\|A\|_{L^{6}_{t}}\exp\left(C\int_{0}^{t}A(\tau)^{2}d\tau\right)\right).

The
L^{p}
estimates of
\psi
, for
p\in(2,\infty)
can be easily obtained by interpolation. It is worthy to point out from (
4.42
), (
4.49
) and (
4.58
) that,

{\mathbb{V}(t)}\leqslant C_{\sigma,m}\implies\min\Big\{\gamma(t),\Theta_{1}(t),\Theta_{2}(t)\Big\}\geqslant 0,

where,

C_{\sigma,m}\triangleq\log\Big(1+\min\Big\{\frac{m-1}{3},\frac{\sigma}{14}\Big\}\Big)>0.

(4.59)

Thereby, the final choice of
T
is given by

[0,T]\triangleq\Big\{t\in[0,T^{\star}):{\mathbb{V}(t)}\leqslant C_{\sigma,m}\;\Big\}.

This ends the proof of the
L^{p}
-estimates of
\nabla\rho
which gives in turn the
L^{p}
-estimates of
\omega
.

It remains to prove the estimate (
4.15
) which will be done in the same spirit as
[
43
]
.
First, Bernstein inequalities and Biot–Savart law allow to get for all
t\in[0,T]

\displaystyle\|v(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{-1}v(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\sum_{j\geqslant 0}\|\Delta_{j}v(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}

(4.60)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{-1}v(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\sum_{j\geqslant 0}2^{-j}\|\Delta_{j}\omega(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{-1}v(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\omega(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Now, in view of the estimate (
4.12
) the problem boils down to estimate the low frequencies of
v(t)
. Applying
\Delta_{-1}
to the velocity equation and taking the
L^{\infty}
norm yields in view of Bernstein inequalities

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{-1}v(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{-1}v_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\|\Delta_{-1}(v\cdot\nabla v)(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\Delta_{-1}\nabla p(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\Delta_{-1}\rho(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}}\Big)d\tau

(4.61)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|v_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\int_{0}^{t}\|(v\cdot\nabla v)(\tau)\|_{L^{q}}+\|\nabla p(\tau)\|_{L^{q}}d\tau+t\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}},

where
q\in[2,\infty)
and we have used the fact that

\|\Delta_{-1}\rho(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leqslant\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}},\quad\forall\tau\in[0,t].

On the other hand, due to the divergence-free of
v
, we may write

\nabla p=-\nabla\Delta^{-1}\text{div}(v\cdot\nabla v)+\nabla\Delta^{-1}\partial_{2}\rho.

Thus, we obtain,

\displaystyle\|(v\cdot\nabla v)(\tau)\|_{L^{q}}+\|\nabla p(\tau)\|_{L^{q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|v(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\omega(\tau)\|_{L^{q}}+\|\rho(\tau)\|_{L^{q}}

(4.62)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|v(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\omega(\tau)\|_{L^{q}}+\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}}.

Gathering (
4.60
), (
4.61
) and (
4.62
) yields

\|v(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leqslant C\|v_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+Ct\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}}+C\|\omega(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+C\int_{0}^{t}\|v(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\omega(\tau)\|_{L^{q}}d\tau.

Gronwall lemma implies

\|v(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\Big((1+t)C_{0}+\|\omega(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\Big)e^{C\int_{0}^{t}\|\omega(\tau)\|_{L^{q}}d\tau}.

(4.63)

On the other hand, from Proposition
4.1
, we have

\|\omega(t)\|_{L^{q}\cap L^{\infty}}\leqslant G(t),

where,
G(t)
is given by (
4.13
) and satisfies, up to a suitable modification in
C_{0}
,

G(t)\geqslant C_{0}(1+t).

Implementing these estimates in (
4.63
) concludes the proof of (
4.15
).

The proof of the last point of Proposition
4.1
concerning the blowup criterion can be achieved following exactly the same argument as in the proof of Corollary
3.1
.

∎

4.2.2.
Co-normal regularity persistence

The next purpose that we want to achieve is to track the striated regularity of the vorticity in the the scale of Besov spaces
B^{s}_{p,\infty}
, with finite
p
. For this aim, the a priori estimates stated in Proposition
4.1
are the cornerstone in dealing with the regularity persistence issue. The first result reads as follows.

Proposition 4.2
.

Let
(s,\varepsilon,h,p)\in(0,1)\times(0,\frac{s}{2})\times(0,e^{-1}]\times[1,\infty)
. Consider a compact negligible set
\Sigma_{0}
of the plane satisfying (
4.9
) and a vector field
X_{0}
of class
B^{s}_{p,\infty}
as well as its divergence and whose support is embedded in
(\Sigma_{0})_{h}^{c}
. Let
(\omega,\rho)
be a smooth maximal solution of the system (
4.1
) defined on
[0,T^{\star})
with initial data
(\omega_{0},\rho_{0})
satisfying the hypothesis of Proposition
4.1
. Let
X_{t}
be the solution of the transport equation

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\big(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla\big)X_{t}=\partial_{X_{t}}v,&\\
X_{|t=0}=X_{0}\end{array}\right.

and introduce

\sigma_{t}\triangleq s\Big(1-\frac{\varepsilon t}{sT}\Big)-\varepsilon,

(4.64)

with

T=\sup\Big\{t\leqslant\min\{1,T^{\star}\}:\;\mathbb{V}(t)\leqslant C_{\sigma,m}\Big\},

(4.65)

where
\mathbb{V}(t)
and
C_{\sigma,m}
are given by (
4.11
) and (
4.59
), respectively.
Then, the following holds for
all
t\in[0,T]

\widetilde{\|}X_{t}\|_{B^{\sigma_{t}}_{p,\infty}}+\|\partial_{X_{t}}\omega(t)\|_{B^{\sigma_{t}-1}_{p,\infty}}\lesssim\widetilde{\zeta}_{0}h^{-Ct\widetilde{W}^{2}(t)}

(4.66)

and

\|\partial_{X_{t}}\rho(t)\|_{B^{\sigma_{t}}_{p,\infty}}\lesssim\widetilde{\zeta}_{0}h^{-C\widetilde{W}^{2}(t)},

(4.67)

with

\widetilde{\zeta}_{0}\triangleq\widetilde{\|}X_{0}\|_{B^{s}_{p,\infty}}+\|\partial_{X_{0}}\omega_{0}\|_{B^{s-1}_{p,\infty}}+\|\partial_{X_{0}}\rho_{0}\|_{B^{s}_{p,\infty}},

\widetilde{W}(t)\triangleq\Big(\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}\|v(\tau)\|_{L(\Sigma_{\tau})}+G(t)\Big)\exp\bigg(\int_{0}^{t}\|v(\tau)\|_{LL}d\tau\bigg)

(4.68)

and
G(t)
is given by (
4.13
).

Remark 4.2
.

The assumption
T\leqslant 1
is not essential. It is merely required to avoid the time growth and get simpler a priori estimates. Remind that we are dealing with local well-posedness issue and the global well-posedness is still an open problem.

Proof of Proposition
4.2
.

First, let us point out that defining
T
by (
4.65
) insures, in view of Proposition
4.1

\|\nabla\rho(t)\|_{L^{2}\cap L^{\infty}}+\|\omega(t)\|_{L^{2}\cap L^{\infty}}\leqslant G(t),\quad\forall t\in[0,T],

(4.69)

where
G(t)
is given by (
4.13
).
This implies that
W(t)
which is given by (
2.12
) satisfies

W(t)\leqslant\widetilde{W}(t),\quad\forall t\in[0,T].

Consequently, Corollary
2.2
yields for all
t\in[0,T]

\textnormal{supp}X_{t}\subset\big(\Sigma_{t}\big)_{\delta_{t}(h)}^{c}\quad\textnormal{with}\quad\delta_{t}(h)=h^{\exp\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|v(\tau)\|_{LL}d\tau\big)},

\|\textnormal{div }X_{t}\|_{B^{s}_{p,\infty}}\leqslant\|\textnormal{div }X_{0}\|_{B^{s}_{p,\infty}}h^{-C\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau)d\tau}

(4.70)

and

\displaystyle\widetilde{\|}X_{t}\|_{B^{\sigma_{t}}_{p,\infty}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle h^{-C\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau)d\tau}\Big(\widetilde{\|}X_{0}\|_{B^{s}_{p,\infty}}+C\int_{0}^{t}h^{C\int_{0}^{\tau}\widetilde{W}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}}\|\partial_{X_{\tau}}\omega(\tau)\|_{B^{\sigma_{\tau}-1}_{p,\infty}}d\tau\Big).

(4.71)

Now, to control the stratiad regularity of the vorticity, we apply the directional derivative
\partial_{X_{t}}
to the equation of
\omega
in (
4.1
) to obtain

\big(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla\big)\partial_{X_{t}}\omega=\partial_{X_{t}}\partial_{1}\rho.

(4.72)

It is easy to see that

\partial_{X_{t}}\partial_{1}\rho=\partial_{1}\partial_{X_{t}}\rho-\partial_{\partial_{1}X_{t}}\rho

and the estimate of the second term on the r.h.s follows from Corollary
2.1
and the fact
that
\sigma_{\tau}\in(0,1)

\|\partial_{\partial_{1}X_{\tau}}\rho\|_{B^{\sigma_{\tau}-1}_{p,\infty}}\lesssim\|\nabla\rho\|_{L^{\infty}}\widetilde{\|}X_{\tau}\|_{B^{\sigma_{\tau}}_{p,\infty}}.

Hence, applying Proposition
2.2
to (
4.72
) gives

\displaystyle\|\partial_{X_{t}}\omega\|_{B^{\sigma_{t}-1}_{p,\infty}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\|\partial_{X_{0}}\omega_{0}\|_{B^{\sigma_{t}-1}_{p,\infty}}h^{-C\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau)d\tau}+\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial_{X_{\tau}}\rho(\tau)\|_{B^{\sigma_{\tau}}_{p,\infty}}h^{-C\int_{\tau}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}}d\tau

\displaystyle+C\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla\rho(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}}\widetilde{\|}X_{\tau}\|_{B^{\sigma_{\tau}}_{p,\infty}}h^{-C\int_{\tau}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}}d\tau.

Next, the elementary inequalities below

\|\nabla\rho(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leqslant G(\tau)\leqslant\widetilde{W}(\tau),\quad\forall\tau\in[0,T],

combined with the fact that
\sigma_{t}\leqslant s
, allow to get

\displaystyle\|\partial_{X_{t}}\omega\|_{B^{\sigma_{t}-1}_{p,\infty}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\|\partial_{X_{0}}\omega_{0}\|_{B^{s-1}_{p,\infty}}h^{-C\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau)d\tau}+\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial_{X_{\tau}}\rho(\tau)\|_{B^{\sigma_{\tau}}_{p,\infty}}h^{-C\int_{\tau}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}}d\tau

(4.73)

\displaystyle+C\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau)\widetilde{\|}X_{\tau}\|_{B^{\sigma_{\tau}}_{p,\infty}}h^{-C\int_{\tau}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}}d\tau.

Hence, gathering this estimate with (
4.71
) yields

\displaystyle\zeta(t)

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\zeta_{0}+C\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau)\zeta(\tau)d\tau+\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial_{X_{\tau}}\rho(\tau)\|_{B^{\sigma_{\tau}}_{p,\infty}}h^{C\int_{0}^{\tau}\widetilde{W}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}}d\tau,

(4.74)

where we use the fact
1\leqslant\widetilde{W}(t)
and the following notation

\zeta(t)\triangleq\left(\widetilde{\|}X_{\cdot}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}(B^{\sigma_{\cdot}}_{p,\infty})}+\|\partial_{X_{\cdot}}\omega\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}(B^{\sigma_{\cdot}-1}_{p,\infty})}\right)h^{C\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau)d\tau},\quad\zeta_{0}=\zeta(0)

(4.75)

and

\widetilde{\|}X_{\cdot}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}(B^{\sigma_{\cdot}}_{p,\infty})}\triangleq\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}\widetilde{\|}X_{\tau}\|_{B^{\sigma_{\tau}}_{p,\infty}},\quad\|\partial_{X_{\cdot}}\omega\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}(B^{\sigma_{\cdot}}_{p,\infty})}\triangleq\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}\|\partial_{X_{\tau}}\omega\|_{B^{\sigma_{\tau}}_{p,\infty}}.

(4.76)

The problem boils down to estimate
\|\partial_{X_{t}}\rho(t)\|_{B^{\sigma_{t}}_{p,\infty}}
. To do so, we apply the directional derivative
\partial_{X_{t}}
to
\rho-
equation to find that

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\big(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla-\kappa\Delta\big)\partial_{X_{t}}\rho=-\kappa[\Delta,\partial_{X_{t}}]\rho&\\
\partial_{X_{t}}\rho_{|_{t=0}}=\partial_{X_{0}}\rho_{0}.\end{array}\right.

(4.77)

Above,
[\Delta,\partial_{X_{t}}]
designates the commutator between
\Delta
and
\partial_{X_{t}}
which can be split in view of Bony’s decomposition in the following way, see for instance
[
23
,
41
]

\kappa[\Delta,\partial_{X_{t}}]={\bf F}+\kappa{\bf G},

where

\displaystyle{\bf F}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\kappa R(\nabla X^{i}_{t},\partial_{i}\nabla\rho)+\kappa R(\Delta X^{i}_{t},\partial_{i}\rho)

and

\displaystyle{\bf G}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2T_{\nabla X^{i}_{t}}\partial_{i}\nabla\rho+2T_{\partial_{i}\nabla\rho}\nabla X^{i}_{t}+T_{\Delta X^{i}_{t}}\partial_{i}\rho+T_{\partial_{i}\rho}\Delta X^{i}_{t}.

Hence, applying the estimate (
2.23
) from Proposition
2.4
yields

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{q}\partial_{X_{t}}\rho(t)\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{q}\partial_{X_{0}}\rho_{0}\|_{L^{p}}+\|{\Delta_{q}{\bf F}}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{p}}+C(1+\kappa t)2^{-2q}\|{\Delta_{q}{\bf G}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{p}}

(4.78)

\displaystyle-\log h\int_{0}^{t}\frac{2^{-q\sigma_{\tau}}}{1-\sigma_{\tau}^{2}}\|\partial_{X_{\tau}}\rho(\tau)\|_{B^{\sigma_{\tau}}_{p,\infty}}W(\tau)d\tau.

The estimates of the terms in
{\bf G}
follow by applying Lemma
2.3
and (
4.69
)

\displaystyle 2^{(\sigma_{t}-2)q}\|{\Delta_{q}{\bf G}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{p}}\leqslant\|{\bf G}\|_{\widetilde{L}^{\infty}_{t}B^{\sigma_{t}-2}_{p,\infty}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|{\bf G}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}B^{\sigma_{t}-2}_{p,\infty}}

(4.79)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\nabla\rho\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{\infty}}\|X_{\cdot}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}B^{\sigma_{t}}_{p,\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle G(t)\|X_{\cdot}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}B^{\sigma_{\cdot}}_{p,\infty}},

where we used in the last line the non-increasing property of
t\mapsto\sigma_{t}
which allows to get

\|X_{\cdot}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}(B^{\sigma_{t}}_{p,\infty})}\triangleq\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}\|X_{\tau}\|_{B^{\sigma_{t}}_{p,\infty}}\leqslant\|X_{\cdot}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}(B^{\sigma_{\cdot}}_{p,\infty})}.

Now, we estimate
{\bf F}
by exploring the smoothing-effect estimates stated in
Corollary
2.3
. First, we transform
{\bf F}
in the following form by using Leibniz rule

\displaystyle{\bf F}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\kappa\partial_{i}R(\nabla X^{i}_{t},\nabla\rho)+2\kappa\partial_{i}R(\Delta X^{i}_{t},\rho)-2\kappa R(\nabla\text{div}X_{t},\nabla\rho)-2\kappa R(\Delta\text{div}X_{t},\rho).

Then, Bernstein Lemma implies

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{q}{\bf F}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 2^{q}\kappa\sum_{j\geqslant q-5}\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{j}X_{\cdot}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{p}}2^{j}\|\Delta_{j}\nabla\rho\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{\infty}}

(4.80)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\kappa\sum_{j\geqslant q-5}\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{j}\textnormal{div }X_{\cdot}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{p}}2^{j}\|\Delta_{j}\nabla\rho\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{\infty}}.

(4.81)

On the other hand, Corollary
2.3
together with the definition of
T
in (
4.65
) insures that

\displaystyle\kappa 2^{2q}\|\Delta_{q}(\partial_{i}\rho)\|_{L_{t}^{1}L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle G(t)\Big(1+\kappa t+(q+2)\int_{0}^{t}\|v(\tau)\|_{LL}d\tau\Big)

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle G(t)(q+2),

Thus, plugging this inequality in (
4.80
) yields for all
t\in[0,T]

\displaystyle 2^{q\sigma_{t}}\|{\Delta_{q}{\bf F}}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{p}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle 2^{q(\sigma_{t}+1)}G(t)\sum_{j\geqslant q-5}2^{-j}(j+2)\Big(\|\Delta_{j}X_{\cdot}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{p}}+\|\Delta_{j}\text{div}X_{\cdot}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{p}}\Big).

(4.82)

Therefore, by using (
4.79
) and (
4.82
) in (
4.78
) together with the fact that
\sigma_{t}\leqslant s
we obtain, for any
\kappa\in(0,1)
and
t\in[0,T]

\displaystyle 2^{q\sigma_{t}}\|\Delta_{q}\partial_{X_{t}}\rho(t)\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\|\partial_{X_{0}}\rho_{0}\|_{B^{s}_{p,\infty}}+C(-\log h)\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial_{X_{\tau}}\rho(\tau)\|_{B^{\sigma_{\tau}}_{p,\infty}}\widetilde{W}(\tau)d\tau

(4.83)

\displaystyle+G(t)\|X_{\cdot}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}B^{\sigma_{\cdot}}_{p,\infty}}

\displaystyle+G(t)2^{q(\sigma_{t}+1)}\sum_{j\geqslant q-5}2^{-j}(j+2)\Big(\|\Delta_{j}X_{\cdot}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{p}}+\|\Delta_{j}\text{div}X_{\cdot}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{p}}\Big).

In order to estimate the term
\|X_{\cdot}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}B^{\sigma_{\cdot}}_{p,\infty}}
, we make use of (
4.71
) to find

\displaystyle\|X_{\cdot}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}B^{\sigma_{\cdot}}_{p,\infty}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle h^{-C\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau)d\tau}\Big(\widetilde{\|}X_{0}\|_{B^{s}_{p,\infty}}+C\int_{0}^{t}h^{C\int_{0}^{\tau}\widetilde{W}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}}\|\partial_{X_{\tau}}\omega(\tau)\|_{B^{\sigma_{\tau}-1}_{p,\infty}}d\tau\Big).

(4.84)

Now, we deal with
\textnormal{div }X_{\cdot}
that appears in the last line of (
4.83
) by exploiting (
4.70
) and the definition of
\sigma_{t}
to achieve

2^{q\sigma_{\tau}}\|\Delta_{j}\text{div}X_{\tau}\|_{L^{p}}\lesssim 2^{-j\varepsilon}\|\text{div}X_{0}\|_{B^{s}_{p,\infty}}h^{-C\int_{0}^{\tau}\widetilde{W}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}}.

Then, the fact
h\in(0,e^{-1}]
and the monotonicity of
\sigma_{t}
imply

2^{q(\sigma_{t}+1)}\sum_{j\geqslant q-5}\|\Delta_{j}\textnormal{div }X_{\cdot}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{p}}2^{-j}(j+2)\lesssim\|\text{div}X_{0}\|_{B^{s}_{p,\infty}}h^{-C\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau)d\tau}.

(4.85)

We are left with the estimate of
\|\Delta_{j}X_{\cdot}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{p}}
in the last line of (
4.83
). First, it follows from (
2.18
) and the fact
W(t)\leqslant\widetilde{W}(t)
that

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{j}X_{t}\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{j}X_{0}\|_{L^{p}}+C2^{-js}\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau)\|\text{div}X_{\tau}\|_{B^{s}_{p,\infty}}d\tau+C\int_{0}^{t}2^{-j\sigma_{\tau}}\|\partial_{X_{\tau}}\omega\|_{B^{\sigma_{\tau}-1}_{p,\infty}}d\tau

(4.86)

\displaystyle+C(-\log h)\int_{0}^{t}2^{-j\sigma_{\tau}}\|X_{\tau}\|_{B^{\sigma_{\tau}}_{p,\infty}}\widetilde{W}(\tau)d\tau.

We will treat each term on the r.h.s of (
4.86
) separately. To begin with, owing to the definition of
\sigma_{t}
given by (
4.64
), we have

\|\Delta_{j}X_{0}\|_{L^{p}}\leqslant 2^{-j(\sigma_{t}+\varepsilon)}\|X_{0}\|_{B^{s}_{p,\infty}}.

Hence, we get

2^{q(\sigma_{t}+1)}\sum_{j\geqslant q-5}(j+2)2^{-j}\|\Delta_{j}X_{0}\|_{L^{p}}\leqslant C\|X_{0}\|_{B^{s}_{p,\infty}}.

(4.87)

Next, to estimate the second term on the r.h.s of (
4.86
), we use again (
4.70
) together with the fact that
1\leqslant-\log h
to obtain

\displaystyle C\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau)\|\text{div}X_{\tau}\|_{B^{s}_{p,\infty}}d\tau

\displaystyle\leqslant-C\log h\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau)h^{-C\int_{0}^{\tau}\widetilde{W}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}}d\tau\|\text{div}X_{0}\|_{B^{s}_{p,\infty}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\text{div}X_{0}\|_{B^{s}_{p,\infty}}h^{-C\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau)d\tau}

(4.88)

On the other hand, the definition of
\sigma_{t}
yields

2^{-js}(j+2)\leqslant 2^{-j\sigma_{t}}\frac{j+2}{2^{j\varepsilon}}\lesssim 2^{-j\sigma_{t}}.

(4.89)

Hence, (
4.2.2
) and (
4.89
) imply that

\displaystyle C2^{q(\sigma_{t}+1)}\sum_{j\geqslant q-5}2^{-js}(j+2)2^{-j}\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau)\|\text{div}X_{\tau}\|_{B^{s}_{p,\infty}}d\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\text{div}X_{0}\|_{B^{s}_{p,\infty}}h^{-C\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau)d\tau}.

(4.90)

Finally, the last two terms in (
4.86
) can be treated simultaneously by using the computation

(j+2)\int_{0}^{t}2^{-j\sigma_{\tau}}d\tau\lesssim\;\frac{j+2}{j}\;2^{-j\sigma_{t}},\quad\forall j\geqslant 1,

to obtain, for
t\in[0,T]

\displaystyle 2^{q(\sigma_{t}+1)}\sum_{j\geqslant q-5}2^{-j}(j+2)\int_{0}^{t}2^{-j\sigma_{\tau}}\|\partial_{X_{\tau}}\omega\|_{B^{\sigma_{\tau}-1}_{p,\infty}}d\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\partial_{X_{\cdot}}\omega\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}B^{\sigma_{\cdot}-1}_{p,\infty}}

(4.91)

and

2^{q(\sigma_{t}+1)}\sum_{j\geqslant q-5}2^{-j}(j+2)\int_{0}^{t}2^{-j\sigma_{\tau}}\|X_{\tau}\|_{B^{\sigma_{\tau}}_{p,\infty}}\widetilde{W}(\tau)d\tau\lesssim\widetilde{W}(t)\|X_{\cdot}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}B^{\sigma_{\cdot}}_{p,\infty}},

(4.92)

where we have used the fact that
t\mapsto\widetilde{W}(t)
is increasing.
Now, gathering (
4.87
), (
4.90
), (
4.91
) and (
4.92
) with (
4.86
) lead to

\displaystyle 2^{q(\sigma_{t}+1)}\sum_{j\geqslant q-5}2^{-j}(j+2)\|\Delta_{j}X_{t}\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\widetilde{\|}X_{0}\|_{B^{s}_{p,\infty}}h^{-C\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau)d\tau}

(4.93)

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{X_{\cdot}}\omega\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}B^{\sigma_{\cdot}-1}_{p,\infty}}+(-\log h)\widetilde{W}(t)\|X_{\cdot}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}B^{\sigma_{\cdot}}_{p,\infty}}.

Plugging (
4.84
), (
4.85
) and (
4.93
) in (
4.83
), together with the facts
-\log h\geqslant 1
and
\widetilde{W}(t)\geqslant 1
yield

\displaystyle\|\partial_{X_{t}}\rho(t)\|_{B^{\sigma_{t}}_{p,\infty}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\|\partial_{X_{0}}\rho_{0}\|_{B^{s}_{p,\infty}}+C(-\log h)\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial_{X_{\tau}}\rho(\tau)\|_{B^{\sigma_{\tau}}_{p,\infty}}\widetilde{W}(\tau)d\tau

\displaystyle+G(t)h^{-C\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau)d\tau}\Bigg(\widetilde{\|}X_{0}\|_{B^{s}_{p,\infty}}+\int_{0}^{t}h^{C\int_{0}^{\tau}\widetilde{W}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}}\|\partial_{X_{\tau}}\omega(\tau)\|_{B^{\sigma_{\tau}-1}_{p,\infty}}d\tau\Bigg)

\displaystyle+G(t)\|\partial_{X_{\cdot}}\omega\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}B^{\sigma_{\cdot}-1}_{p,\infty}}+(-\log h)\widetilde{W}(t)G(t)\|X_{\cdot}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}B^{\sigma_{\cdot}}_{p,\infty}}.

Thereafter, in view of the notation (
4.75
), we use (
4.84
) together with (
4.73
), to end up with

\displaystyle\|\partial_{X_{t}}\rho(t)\|_{B^{\sigma_{t}}_{p,\infty}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\|\partial_{X_{0}}\rho_{0}\|_{B^{s}_{p,\infty}}-\log h\;G(t)\widetilde{W}(t)h^{-C\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau)d\tau}\zeta_{0}

\displaystyle+(-\log h)\;G(t)\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau)h^{-C\int_{\tau}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}}\|\partial_{X_{\tau}}\rho(\tau)\|_{B^{\sigma_{\tau}}_{p,\infty}}d\tau

\displaystyle+(-\log h)\;G(t)\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau)h^{-C\int_{\tau}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}}\Big(\|\partial_{X_{\tau}}\omega(\tau)\|_{B^{\sigma_{\tau}-1}_{p,\infty}}+\widetilde{\|}X_{\tau}\|_{B^{\sigma_{\tau}}_{p,\infty}}\Big)d\tau.

Consequently, we get

\displaystyle h^{C\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau)d\tau}\|\partial_{X_{t}}\rho(t)\|_{B^{\sigma_{t}}_{p,\infty}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle(-\log h)\;G(t)\widetilde{W}(t)\widetilde{\zeta}_{0}

\displaystyle+(-\log h)\;G(t)\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau)h^{C\int_{0}^{\tau}\widetilde{W}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}}\|\partial_{X_{\tau}}\rho(\tau)\|_{B^{\sigma_{\tau}}_{p,\infty}}d\tau

\displaystyle+(-\log h)\;G(t)\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau)\zeta(\tau)d\tau.

Applying Gronwall lemma leads then to

\displaystyle h^{C\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau)d\tau}\|\partial_{X_{t}}\rho(t)\|_{B^{\sigma_{t}}_{p,\infty}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle(-\log h)\;G(t)\widetilde{W}(t)\widetilde{\zeta}_{0}h^{-G(t)\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}}

(4.94)

\displaystyle+(-\log h)\;G(t)\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau)h^{-G(t)\int_{\tau}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}}\zeta(\tau)d\tau.

An integration with respect to
t
together with the computation

\displaystyle(-\log h)\;\int_{0}^{t}G(\tau)\widetilde{W}(\tau)h^{-G(\tau)\int_{0}^{\tau}\widetilde{W}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}}d\tau

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle(-\log h)\;G(t)\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau)h^{-G(t)\int_{0}^{\tau}\widetilde{W}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}}d\tau

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle h^{-G(t)\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}}

leads to

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}h^{C\int_{0}^{\tau}\widetilde{W}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}}\|\partial_{X_{\tau}}\rho(\tau)\|_{B^{\sigma_{\tau}}_{p,\infty}}d\tau\leqslant\widetilde{\zeta}_{0}h^{-G(t)\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad+(-\log h)\int_{0}^{t}G(\tau)d\tau\Bigg(\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau)h^{-G(t)\int_{\tau}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}}\zeta(\tau)d\tau\Bigg).

Plugging this inequality in (
4.74
) and using the fact that
1\leqslant G(t)
yield

\displaystyle\zeta(t)

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\widetilde{\zeta}_{0}h^{-G(t)\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}}

\displaystyle+(-\log h)\Big(1+\int_{0}^{t}G(\tau)d\tau\Big)\Bigg(\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau)h^{-G(t)\int_{\tau}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}}\zeta(\tau)d\tau\Bigg).

Next, by setting

\Gamma(t)\triangleq h^{G(t)\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}}\zeta(t)

we deduce that

\displaystyle\Gamma(t)

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\widetilde{\zeta}_{0}+(-\log h)G(t)\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau)\Gamma(\tau)d\tau,

where we have used the the inequality

\int_{0}^{t}G(\tau)d\tau\leqslant G(t),\quad\forall\;t\in[0,T]

which follows from the increasing property of
t\mapsto G(t)
and the fact
T\leqslant 1.

Gronwall lemma yields

\displaystyle\Gamma(t)

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\widetilde{\zeta}_{0}h^{-G(t)\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau)d\tau}.

(4.95)

On the other hand, by the monotonicity of
t\mapsto\widetilde{W}(t)
and the definitions (
4.13
) and (
4.68
), which insure that
G(t)\leqslant\widetilde{W}(t)
, we end up with

\displaystyle\widetilde{\|}X_{t}\|_{B^{\sigma_{t}}_{p,\infty}}+\|\partial_{X_{t}}\omega(t)\|_{B^{\sigma_{t}-1}_{p,\infty}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\widetilde{\zeta}_{0}h^{-Ct\widetilde{W}^{2}(t)}.

(4.96)

We are left now with the estimate of
\partial_{X_{t}}\rho
. First, by inserting (
4.95
) in (
4.94
), we deduce that

\displaystyle h^{C\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau)d\tau}\|\partial_{X_{t}}\rho(t)\|_{B^{\sigma_{t}}_{p,\infty}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle(-\log h)\;G(t)\widetilde{W}(t)\widetilde{\zeta}_{0}h^{-G(t)\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}}

\displaystyle+h^{-G(t)\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}}(-\log h)\;G(t)\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau)\Gamma(\tau)d\tau

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle(-\log h)\;G(t)\widetilde{W}(t)\widetilde{\zeta}_{0}h^{-G(t)\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}}

\displaystyle+(-\log h)\;\widetilde{\zeta}_{0}G(t)\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau)h^{-G(t)\int_{0}^{\tau}\widetilde{W}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}}d\tau.

Then, straightforward computation yields

\displaystyle\|\partial_{X_{t}}\rho(t)\|_{B^{\sigma_{t}}_{p,\infty}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle(-\log h)\;\widetilde{\zeta}_{0}\widetilde{W}^{2}(t)h^{-G(t)\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\widetilde{\zeta}_{0}h^{-C\widetilde{W}^{2}(t)}.

Proposition
4.2
is then proved.
∎

4.2.3.
Uniform time existence

Our purpose now is to establish suitable a priori estimates on some interval
[0,T]
where
T>0
is related to the initial data covering singular vortex patches and it is independent of
\kappa\in[0,1].

Our result is stated as follows.

Proposition 4.3
.

Let
(s,\varepsilon,p)\in(0,1)\times(0,\frac{s}{2})\times(\frac{1}{\frac{s}{2}-\varepsilon},\infty)
, and
m\in(1,2)
. Let
\Sigma_{0}
be a negligible compact set of the plane satisfying (
4.9
) and consider
\mathcal{X}_{0}=\big(X_{0,\lambda,h}\big)_{\lambda\in\Lambda,h\in(0,e^{-1}]}
to be a family of
\Sigma_{0}
-admissible vector fields of order
\Xi_{0}=(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)
and of class
B^{s}_{p,\infty}
as well as their divergence. Let
(\omega,\rho)
be a maximal smooth solution of the system (
4.1
) defined on a time interval
[0,T^{\star})
with the initial data
(\omega_{0},\rho_{0})
satisfying

\omega_{0}\in L^{2}\cap L^{\infty},\quad\rho_{0}\in W^{2,2}\cap W^{2,\infty},

\sup_{x\notin\Sigma_{0}}\big(\varphi_{0}(x)\big)^{-m}\left|\nabla\rho_{0}(x)\right|<\infty,\quad\quad\sup_{x\notin\Sigma_{0}}\big(\varphi_{0}(x)\big)^{-m+1}\left|\nabla|\nabla\rho_{0}(x)|^{2}\right|<\infty

and

\sup_{h\in(0,e^{-1}]}h^{\beta}\Big(\|\omega_{0}\|_{(\Sigma_{0})_{h},(\mathcal{X}_{0})}^{s,p}+\|\rho_{0}\|_{(\Sigma_{0})_{h},(\mathcal{X}_{0})}^{s+1,p}\Big)<\infty,

where
\varphi_{0}
is given by (
2.4
).
Then, there exists
T\in(0,T^{\star})
such that

t\in[0,T]\mapsto\|v(t)\|_{L(\Sigma_{t})\cap LL\cap L^{\infty}}\in L^{\infty}\big([0,T]\big)

and

(\omega,\rho)\in L^{\infty}\big([0,T],L^{2}\cap L^{\infty}\big)\times L^{\infty}\big([0,T],W^{1,2}\cap W^{1,\infty}\big).

In addition, by setting

\sigma_{t}=s\Big(1-\frac{\varepsilon t}{sT}\Big)-\varepsilon,

there exist some continuous functions
\Xi(t)=(\alpha(t),\beta(t),\gamma(t))
and
C_{0,T}>0
, depending only on
T
and the initial data such that

{\sup_{h\in(0,e^{-1}]}h^{\beta(t)}\|\omega(t)\|_{(\Sigma_{t})_{\delta_{t}^{-1}(h)},(\mathcal{X}_{t,h})}^{\sigma_{t}}+\sup_{h\in(0,e^{-1}]}h^{\beta(t)}\|\rho(t)\|_{(\Sigma_{t})_{\delta_{t}^{-1}(h)},(\mathcal{X}_{t,h})}^{\sigma_{t}+1}\leqslant C_{0,T},}

where,
\delta_{t}^{-1}(h)
is defined in (
2.20
) and
\Sigma_{t}\triangleq\Psi(t,\Sigma_{0}).

Moreover, the transported vector-fields
\mathcal{X}_{t}
of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
by the flow defined through
\Psi

t\mapsto X_{t,\lambda,h}(\cdot)\triangleq\partial_{X_{0,\lambda,h}}\Psi(t,\Psi^{-1}(t,\cdot)),

is
\Sigma_{t}
-admissible of order
\Xi(t)
and belongs to
L^{\infty}([0,T];B^{\sigma_{t}}_{p,\infty}),
for all
(\lambda,h)\in\Lambda\times(0,e^{-1}].

Proof.

We recall that the results of Proposition
4.1
and Proposition
4.2
are satisfied on the interval
[0,T]
as long as

\mathbb{V}(T)\leqslant C_{\sigma,m},

where
\mathbb{V}(T)
and
C_{\sigma,m}
are given by (
4.11
) and (
4.59
), respectively.
In what follows, we consider a time
T>0
such that

\mathbb{V}(T)\leqslant C_{\sigma,m}.

(4.97)

which is true by a continuity argument, and we shall see at the end that we can get a lower bound for
T
uniformly in
\kappa\in[0,1].

Therefore, by (
4.97
) all the results of Proposition
4.1
and Proposition
4.2
hold on
[0,T]
.
Now, we intend to track the time evolution of the stratified regularity with the related norms. We begin with the admissibility of the family
\mathcal{X}_{t}
. To this end, we apply Proposition
2.3
in order to get

I\big((\Sigma_{t})_{\delta_{t}^{-1}(h)},(\mathcal{X}_{t})_{h}\big)\geqslant I\big((\Sigma_{0})_{h},(\mathcal{X}_{0})_{h}\big)h^{\int_{0}^{t}W(\tau)d\tau}\geqslant I\big((\Sigma_{0})_{h},(\mathcal{X}_{0})_{h}\big)h^{-t\widetilde{W}^{2}(t)},

(4.98)

where we have used the monotonicity of
t\mapsto\widetilde{W}(t)
together with the fact that
W(t)\leqslant\widetilde{W}^{2}(t)
.
Next, we introduce the quantity

\Upsilon(t)\triangleq\|\omega(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\widetilde{\|}X_{t,\lambda,h}\|_{B_{p,\infty}^{\sigma_{t}}}+\|\partial_{X_{t,\lambda,h}}\omega(t)\|_{B_{p,\infty}^{\sigma_{t}-1}}.

Owing to Proposition
4.1
, we have for all
t\in[0,T]

\|\omega(t)\|_{L^{2}\cap L^{\infty}}\leqslant G(t).

(4.99)

Together with the estimate (
4.66
) and the fact that
1\leqslant G(t)
, we obtain

\Upsilon(t)\leqslant\left(\widetilde{\|}X_{0}\|_{B^{s}_{p,\infty}}+\|\partial_{X_{0}}\omega\|_{B^{s-1}_{p,\infty}}+\|\partial_{X_{0}}\rho_{0}\|_{B^{s}_{p,\infty}}\right)G(t)h^{-Ct\widetilde{W}^{2}(t)}.

Hence, Definition
2.5
and (
4.98
) imply

\displaystyle\Upsilon(t)

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\Big(N_{s,p}\big((\Sigma_{0})_{h},(\mathcal{X}_{0})_{h}\big)+\|\omega_{0}\|^{s,p}_{(\Sigma_{0})_{h},(\mathcal{X}_{0})_{h}}+\|\rho_{0}\|^{s+1,p}_{(\Sigma_{0})_{h},(\mathcal{X}_{0})_{h}}\Big)I\big((\Sigma_{0})_{h},(\mathcal{X}_{0})_{h}\big)G(t)h^{-Ct\widetilde{W}^{2}(t)}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle C_{0}I\big((\Sigma_{t})_{\delta_{t}^{-1}(h)},(\mathcal{X}_{t})_{h}\big)G(t)h^{-\beta-Ct\widetilde{W}^{2}(t)},

where
C_{0}
depends only on the initial data and not on
h
. Consequently, in view of Definition
2.4
, we infer that

\|\omega(t)\|^{\sigma_{t},p}_{(\Sigma_{t})_{\delta_{t}^{-1}(h)},(\mathcal{X}_{t})_{h}}\leqslant C_{0}G(t)h^{-\beta-Ct\widetilde{W}^{2}(t)}.

(4.100)

Similar arguments, using (
4.67
) instead of (
4.66
), lead to

\|\rho(t)\|^{\sigma_{t}+1,p}_{(\Sigma_{t})_{\delta_{t}^{-1}(h)},(\mathcal{X}_{t})_{h}}\leqslant C_{0}G(t)h^{-\beta-C\widetilde{W}^{2}(t)}.

(4.101)

On the other hand, due to the fact that

\sigma_{t}\geqslant s-2{\varepsilon},\forall t\in[0,T],

together with the assumption
p>\frac{1}{\frac{s}{2}-\varepsilon}
, we obtain in view of (
4.100
),
\bar{s}\triangleq s-2\varepsilon-\frac{2}{p}\in(0,1)
and Sobolev embeddings

\|\omega(t)\|^{\bar{s}}_{(\Sigma_{t})_{\delta_{t}^{-1}(h)},(\mathcal{X}_{t})_{h}}\lesssim\|\omega(t)\|^{\sigma_{t},p}_{(\Sigma_{t})_{\delta_{t}^{-1}(h)},(\mathcal{X}_{t})_{h}}\leqslant C_{0}G(t)h^{-\beta-Ct\widetilde{W}^{2}(t)}.

(4.102)

Thus, Theorem
2.1
together with (
4.102
) yield

\displaystyle\|\nabla v(t)\|_{L^{\infty}((\Sigma_{t})^{c}_{\delta_{t}^{-1}(h)})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\omega(t)\|_{L^{2}}+\|\omega(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\log\left(e+\tfrac{\|\omega\|^{\bar{s}}_{(\Sigma_{t})_{\delta_{t}^{-1}(h)},(\mathcal{X}_{t})_{h}}}{\|\omega(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle G(t)\log\Big(e+C_{0}h^{-\beta-Ct\widetilde{W}^{2}(t)}\Big),

where we have used the monotonicity of the functions
x\mapsto x\log(e+\frac{b}{x})
and
x\mapsto\log(e+\frac{x}{c})
together with (
4.99
).
Therefore, we deduce for any
t\in[0,T]

\displaystyle\|\nabla v(t)\|_{L^{\infty}((\Sigma_{t})^{c}_{\delta_{t}^{-1}(h)})}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle G(t)\bigg(C_{0}+\beta+Ct\widetilde{W}^{2}(t)\bigg)(-\log h).

Then, using the inequalities

\|v(t)\|_{LL}\lesssim\|\omega(t)\|_{L^{2}\cap L^{\infty}}\lesssim G(t)\leqslant\widetilde{W}(t),\quad\forall t\in[0,T].

(4.103)

together with the definition (
2.20
) yield

\frac{\|\nabla v(t)\|_{L^{\infty}((\Sigma_{t})^{c}_{\delta_{t}^{-1}(h)})}}{-\log(\delta_{t}^{-1}(h))}\leqslant C_{0}G(t)\bigg(1+t\widetilde{W}^{2}(t)\bigg)e^{\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau)d\tau}.

Hence, we obtain

\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}\|v(\tau)\|_{L(\Sigma_{\tau})}\leqslant C_{0}G(t)\bigg(1+t\widetilde{W}^{2}(t)\bigg)e^{\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau)d\tau}.

(4.104)

Consequently, the definition (
4.68
) together with (
4.103
) imply

\widetilde{W}(t)\leqslant C_{0}G(t)\bigg(1+t\widetilde{W}^{2}(t)\bigg)e^{\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{W}(\tau)d\tau}.

(4.105)

On the other hand, from the definition of
G(t)
and by virtue of (
4.103
), we have that

\displaystyle G(t)

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle C_{0}+C_{0}t^{\frac{1}{6}}\widetilde{W}(t)e^{Ct\widetilde{W}^{2}(t)},\quad\forall\,0\leqslant t<\min(1,T^{\star}).

Inserting this inequality into (
4.105
) and using again the facts
\widetilde{W}(t)\geqslant 1
and
t\leqslant 1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\widetilde{W}(t)

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{0}e^{Ct\widetilde{W}^{2}(t)}\Big(1+C_{0}t^{\frac{1}{6}}\widetilde{W}(t)\Big)

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{0}e^{Ct^{\frac{1}{3}}\widetilde{W}^{2}(t)},\quad\forall\,0\leqslant t<\min(1,T^{\star}).

(4.106)

Define

\displaystyle T=\left(\tfrac{\ln 2}{4CC_{0}^{2}}\right)^{3},

(4.107)

which is smaller than
1
since the constants
C
and
C_{0}
can be taken large enough. Then we can check by the continuity of
t\in[0,T^{\star})\mapsto\widetilde{W}(t)
and (
4.2.3
) that

\displaystyle\forall t\in\big[0,\min(T,T^{\star})\big),\quad\widetilde{W}(t)\leqslant 2C_{0}.

(4.108)

It remains to check that
T^{\star}>T
. Without loss of generality, we can assume that
T^{\star}<\infty
. We shall argue by contradiction and assume that
T^{\star}\leqslant T.
According to (
4.16
), we should have

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to T^{\star}}\mathbb{V}(t)>C_{\sigma,m},

(4.109)

where
C_{\sigma,m}
is defined in (
4.59
).
Now, we write by definition

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}(t)=\int_{0}^{t}\|v(\tau)\|_{L(\Sigma_{\tau})\cap LL}d\tau

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle t\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}\Big(\|v(\tau)\|_{L(\Sigma_{\tau})}+\|v(\tau)\|_{LL}\Big)

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle t\widetilde{W}(t).

Thus, using (
4.108
) and (
4.107
) we find

\displaystyle\forall t\in[0,T^{\star}),\quad\mathbb{V}(t)\leqslant

\displaystyle 2TC_{0}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle 2\left(\tfrac{\ln 2}{4C}\right)^{3}C_{0}^{-2}.

Since
C
and
C_{0}
are taken large enough, then the latter inequality contradicts the assumption (
4.109
). Finally, we get that
T^{\star}>T
and the time
T
given by (
4.107
) is only related to the size of the initial data and it is independent of
\kappa\in[0,1].

This ends the proof of Proposition
4.3
.
∎

The next result will be useful in the study of the regularity of the transported initial boundary of the vortex patch stated in Theorem
4.1
.

Corollary 4.1
.

Under the assumptions of Proposition
4.3
, we have

\partial_{X_{0,\lambda,h}}\Psi(t,\cdot)=X_{t,\lambda,h}(\Psi(t,\cdot))\in L^{\infty}_{t}\big([0,T];C^{\sigma_{t}-\frac{2}{p}}\big),\quad\forall(\lambda,h)\in\Lambda\times(0,e^{-1}].

More precisely, there exists a universal constant
C_{0,h,T}>0
depending only on the initial data,
h
and
T
such that, for all
t\in[0,T]

\|X_{t,\lambda,h}(\Psi(t,\cdot))\|_{C^{\sigma_{t}-\frac{2}{p}}}\leqslant C_{0,h,T}.

(4.110)

If moreover,
\textnormal{supp}X_{0,\lambda,h}\subset K_{\lambda,h}\Subset\mathbb{R}^{2},
then one has

\|X_{t,\lambda,h}(\Psi(t,\cdot))\|_{B^{\sigma_{t}-\frac{2}{p}}_{p,\infty}}\leqslant C_{K_{\lambda,h}}C_{0,h,T}.

(4.111)

Proof.

The first part of the proof is similar to the one of Corollary
3.2
. Indeed, by assumptions of Proposition
4.3
, we have
\sigma_{t}-\frac{2}{p}\in(0,1).
Thus, owing to the embedding

B^{\sigma_{t}}_{p,\infty}\hookrightarrow C^{\sigma_{t}-\frac{2}{p}}

and repeating the same arguments in the proof of Corollary
3.2
leads to (
4.110
). The proof of (
4.111
) follows by a direct application of Lemma
2.4
.
∎

4.3.
Proof of Theorem
4.1

The proof of the existence and the uniqueness for the system (
4.1
)is quite similar to that of the inviscid case developed in detail in Section
3.3
. Here we shall only sketch the main lines. First we construct approximate solutions by considering the system

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}\partial_{t}v_{n}+v_{n}\cdot\nabla v_{n}+\nabla p_{n}=\rho_{n}\begin{pmatrix}0\\
1\end{pmatrix},&\\
\partial_{t}\rho_{n}+v_{n}\cdot\nabla\rho_{n}-\kappa\Delta\rho_{n}=0,&\\
\textnormal{div}\,v_{n}=0,\\
v_{0,n}=S_{n}v_{0},\quad\rho_{0,n}=\widetilde{S}_{n}\rho_{0}.\end{array}\right.

(4.112)

where
S_{n}
is the usual cut-off in frequency defined in Section 2, and
\widetilde{S}_{n}
is given by

\widetilde{S}_{n}\rho_{0}\triangleq\chi_{r}\rho_{0}+(1-\chi_{r})S_{n}\rho_{0},

with

\chi_{r}
is a smooth cut-off function with values in
[0,1]
and satisfying

\chi_{r}(x)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1&\text{ if }d(x,\Sigma_{0})\leqslant\frac{r}{2},\\
0&\text{ if }d(x,\Sigma_{0})\geqslant r.\end{array}\right.

Now, by assumption, we have
\chi_{r}\rho_{0}\in C^{1+m}
, where
m\in(1,2).
Also, it is clear that
v_{0,n}
and
(1-\chi_{r})S_{n}\rho_{0}
are smooth and belong to
C^{1+m}
. Hence, the result
[
16
]
applies and implies the existence of a unique smooth solution of (
4.112
). The rest of the proof can be done in a similar way to the inviscid case, however, we need to check that the platitude conditions in Theorem
4.1
imply the assumptions (
4.10
) uniformly in
n
. First, we observe that

\nabla\widetilde{S}_{n}\rho_{0}(y)=\nabla\rho_{0}(y),\quad\forall y\in(\Sigma_{0})_{\frac{r}{2}}.

(4.113)

and consequently we get from straightforward arguments based on the platitude of
\rho_{0}
and the law products

\displaystyle\sup_{\underset{x\neq y,\;|x-y|<{r}/{2}}{y\in\Sigma_{0}}}\frac{|\nabla|\nabla\widetilde{S}_{n}\rho_{0}(x)|^{2}|}{|x-y|^{\alpha-1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{\underset{x\neq y,\;|x-y|<{r}/{2}}{y\in\Sigma_{0}}}\frac{\big|\nabla|\nabla\rho_{0}(x)|^{2}-\nabla|\nabla\rho_{0}(y)|^{2}\big|}{|x-y|^{\alpha-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\nabla|\nabla\rho_{0}|^{2}\|_{C^{\alpha-1}((\Sigma_{0})_{r})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\rho_{0}\|_{C^{\alpha+1}((\Sigma_{0})_{r})}^{2}.

This proves the second condition in (
4.10
) uniformly in
n
. As to the first condition, we write

\displaystyle\sup_{\underset{x\neq y,\;|x-y|<{r}/{2}}{y\in\Sigma_{0}}}\frac{|\nabla\widetilde{S}_{n}\rho(x)|}{|x-y|^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{\underset{x\neq y,\;|x-y|<{r}/{2}}{y\in\Sigma_{0}}}\frac{|\nabla\rho_{0}(x)-\nabla\rho_{0}(y)|}{|x-y|^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\big|\nabla^{2}\rho_{0}(x+t(y-x))-\nabla^{2}\rho_{0}(y)\big|}{|x-y|^{\alpha-1}}dt

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}\rho_{0}\|_{C^{\alpha-1}((\Sigma_{0})_{r})}\lesssim\|\rho_{0}\|_{C^{\alpha+1}((\Sigma_{0})_{r})}.

This ensures the hypothesis (
4.10
) and the a priori estimates of Proposition
4.3
can be used.

5.
Inviscid limit

This section is devoted to the inviscid limit problem for the system (
Bκ
) when the diffusivity parameter goes to zero. This will be implemented in a classical way using energy method combined with Yudovich arguments. Refined convergence results specific to singular vortex patches will be also addressed. The main result can be stated as follows.

Theorem 5.1
.

Let
\kappa\in[0,1]
and
(v_{\kappa},\rho_{\kappa})

(
resp.
(v,\rho))
be a local solution of (
Bκ
)
(
resp. of (
B0
)
)
with initial data
(v_{0,\kappa},\rho_{0,\kappa})

(
resp.
(v_{0},\rho_{0}))
. Let
\Psi_{\kappa}

(
resp.
\Psi)
denote the flow associated with
v_{\kappa}

(
resp.
v)
. Assume that all the solutions are defined on some common interval of time
[0,T]
and we suppose that
v_{0,\kappa}-v_{0}\in L^{2}
and for all
\forall t\in[0,T]

\sup_{\kappa\in[0,1]}\left(\big\|\big(v_{\kappa}(t),v(t)\big)\big\|_{L^{\infty}}+\big\|\big(\omega_{\kappa}(t),\omega(t)\big)\big\|_{L^{2}\cap L^{\infty}}+\big\|\big(\rho_{\kappa}(t),\rho(t)\big)\big\|_{W^{1,2}\cap W^{1,\infty}}\right)\leqslant C_{0,T},

(5.1)

for some constant
C_{0,T}
, with
\omega_{\kappa}=\text{rot }v_{\kappa}
and
\omega=\text{rot }v
. Then, the following results occur for
any
t\in[0,T]

(1)

\|v_{\kappa}(t)-v(t)\|_{L^{2}}+\|\rho_{\kappa}(t)-\rho(t)\|_{L^{2}}\leqslant C_{0,T}\Big(\|v_{0,\kappa}-v_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\rho_{0,\kappa}-\rho_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\kappa t\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}\exp(-TC_{0,T})}
,

(2)

\|\Psi_{\kappa}(t,\cdot)-\Psi(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leqslant C_{0,T}\Big(\|v_{0,\kappa}-v_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\rho_{0,\kappa}-\rho_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\kappa t\Big)^{{\frac{1}{4}\exp(-TC_{0,T})}},

as long as

C_{0,T}\Big(\|v_{0,\kappa}-v_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\rho_{0,\kappa}-\rho_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\kappa t\Big)^{{\frac{1}{2}\exp(-TC_{0,T})}}\lesssim 1.

Moreover, if
\omega_{0,\kappa}=\omega_{0}=\mathrm{1}_{\Omega}
and
\rho_{0,\kappa}=\rho_{0}
satisfy the assumptions in Theorem
1.2
, then the following holds. For any connected component of
\partial\Omega\backslash\Sigma_{0}
, there exists
\gamma^{0}
a
C^{1+s}(\mathbb{R})
-parametrization of this connected component such that

(3)

For all
t\in[0,T]
,
\gamma_{t,\kappa}(\cdot)=\Psi_{\kappa}(t,\gamma^{0}(\cdot))
converges to
\gamma_{t}(\cdot)=\Psi(t,\gamma^{0}(\cdot))
, as
\kappa
goes to zero, in
C^{1+\bar{s}}(\mathbb{R})
, for all
\bar{s}<s
.

Few remarks are in order.

Remark 5.1
.

We point out the fact that
\hbox{curl}v_{0}\in L^{1}\cap L^{\infty}
does not imply in general that
v_{0}\in L^{2}
unless the circulation is zero, that is,
\displaystyle{\int\hbox{curl}}\,v_{0}dx=0.
Hence in
Theorem
5.1
we have to assume initially that
v_{0,\kappa}-v_{0}\in L^{2}
. Such condition is automatically satisfied if for instance
\hbox{curl}\,v_{0,\kappa}=1_{\Omega_{0,\kappa}}
and
\hbox{curl}\,v_{0}=1_{\Omega_{0}}
with
|\Omega_{0,\kappa}|=|\Omega_{0}|
.

Proof.

(1)
The idea of the proof of the first part is based on the Yudovich arguments
[
73
]
and well-developed in
[
4
, Theorem 7.37]
.
Setting
U_{\kappa}=v_{\kappa}-v,\;\vartheta_{\kappa}=\rho_{\kappa}-\rho
and
P_{\kappa}=\pi_{\kappa}-\pi
then we can show from the equations that
(U_{\kappa},\vartheta_{\kappa},P_{\kappa})
satisfies

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\partial_{t}U_{\kappa}+v_{\kappa}\cdot\nabla U_{\kappa}+\nabla P_{\kappa}=\vartheta_{\kappa}e_{2}-U_{\kappa}\cdot\nabla v,\\
\partial_{t}\vartheta_{\kappa}+v_{\kappa}\cdot\nabla\vartheta_{\kappa}-\kappa\Delta\vartheta_{\kappa}=-U_{\kappa}\cdot\nabla\rho+\kappa\Delta\rho,\\
\text{div}\;U_{\kappa}=0,&\\
U_{|t=0}=U_{0,\kappa},\quad\vartheta_{|t=0}=\vartheta_{0,\kappa}.\end{array}\right.

(5.2)

Dotting
U_{\kappa}
-equation (resp.
\vartheta_{\kappa}
-equation) by
U_{\kappa}
(resp.
\vartheta_{\kappa}
) respectively, after some integration by parts, interpolation and the Biot–Savart law we obtain, for any
q\in[2,\infty)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\|U_{\kappa}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\|\vartheta_{\kappa}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\|U_{\kappa}(t)\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla v(t)\|_{L^{q}}\|U_{\kappa}(t)\|_{L^{2q^{\prime}}}^{2}

(5.3)

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\big(\|\vartheta_{\kappa}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|U_{\kappa}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big)+Cq\|\omega(t)\|_{L^{2}}\cap L^{\infty}\|U_{\kappa}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{2}{q^{\prime}}}\|U_{\kappa}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{2}{q}}

and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\|\vartheta_{\kappa}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+

\displaystyle\kappa\|\nabla\vartheta_{\kappa}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leqslant\kappa\|\nabla\rho(t)\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\vartheta_{\kappa}(t)\|_{L^{2}}+\|U_{\kappa}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\rho(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\vartheta_{\kappa}(t)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leqslant\frac{\kappa}{2}\|\nabla\rho(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\kappa}{2}\|\nabla\vartheta_{\kappa}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla\rho(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\big(\|U_{\kappa}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\vartheta_{\kappa}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big),

where
q^{\prime}
is the conjugate exponent of
q
.
Thus, we deduce that the quantity

\zeta_{\kappa}(t)\triangleq\|U_{\kappa}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\vartheta_{\kappa}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

obeys the differential inequality

\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\zeta_{\kappa}(t)\leqslant\kappa C_{0,T}^{2}+\Big(C_{0,T}+1\Big)\zeta_{\kappa}(t)+C_{0,T}^{1+\frac{2}{q}}\,q(\zeta_{\kappa}(t))^{1-\frac{1}{q}},

(5.4)

where we have used the control assumption (
5.1
). In the sequel,
C_{0,T}
may vary from line to another.
Hence, (
5.4
) implies

\frac{d}{dt}\zeta_{\kappa}(t)\leqslant\kappa C_{0,T}^{2}+C_{0}\zeta_{\kappa}(t)+qC_{0,T}^{1+\frac{2}{q}}(\zeta_{\kappa}(t))^{1-\frac{1}{q}}.

(5.5)

Assuming
\zeta_{\kappa}(t)\leqslant 1
on
[0,T]
and
C_{0,T}\geqslant 1
, then (
5.5
) yields

\frac{d}{dt}\zeta_{\kappa}(t)\leqslant\kappa C_{0,T}^{2}+qC_{0,T}^{1+\frac{2}{q}}(\zeta_{\kappa}(t))^{1-\frac{1}{q}}.

Therefore, the quantity

\widetilde{\zeta}_{\kappa}(t)\triangleq\frac{\zeta_{\kappa}(t)}{C_{0,T}^{2}}

satisfies

\frac{d}{dt}\widetilde{\zeta}_{\kappa}(t)\leqslant\kappa+qC_{0,T}(\widetilde{\zeta}_{\kappa}(t))^{1-\frac{1}{q}}.

Thus, with the choice
q=2-2\log\widetilde{\zeta}_{\kappa}(t)
, and up to increase if necessary
C_{0,T}
, we find

\widetilde{\zeta}_{\kappa}(t)\leqslant\widetilde{\zeta}_{\kappa}(0)+\kappa t+C_{0,T}\int_{0}^{t}\widetilde{\zeta}_{\kappa}(\tau)\log\Bigg(\frac{e^{2}}{\widetilde{\zeta}_{\kappa}(\tau)}\Bigg)d\tau.

Applying Osgood Lemma
A.1
yields

\|v_{\kappa}(t)-v(t)\|_{L^{2}}+\|\rho_{\kappa}(t)-\rho(t)\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim C_{0,T}\Big(\|v_{0,\kappa}-v_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\rho_{0,\kappa}-\rho_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\kappa t\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}\exp(-TC_{0,T})}

and the first inequality in Theorem
5.1
follows.

(2)
Let us now prove the convergence of the flow. We write first, for all
t\in[0,T]

\Psi(t,x)-\Psi_{\kappa}(t,x)=\int_{0}^{t}v(\tau,\Psi(\tau,x))-v_{\kappa}(\tau,\Psi_{\kappa}(\tau,x))d\tau.

This yields

\displaystyle\|\Psi(t,\cdot)-\Psi_{\kappa}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\|v(\tau,\Psi(\tau,\cdot))-v(\tau,\Psi_{\kappa}(\tau,\cdot))\|_{L^{\infty}}d\tau

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\|v(\tau,\Psi_{\kappa}(\tau,\cdot))-v_{\kappa}(\tau,\Psi_{\kappa}(\tau,\cdot))\|_{L^{\infty}}d\tau.

For the first term on the r.h.s, we use the fact that
v
is log-Lipschitz, which follows from the assumption
\omega\in L^{2}\cap L^{\infty}
, together with the increasing property of the function
x\mapsto x\log(\frac{e}{x})
,
x\in(0,1)
. Then interpolation argument leads to

\displaystyle\|\Psi(t,\cdot)-\Psi_{\kappa}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\|v(\tau)\|_{LL}\|\Psi(\tau,\cdot)-\Psi_{\kappa}(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}}\log\Bigg(\frac{e}{\|\Psi(\tau,\cdot)-\Psi_{\kappa}(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}}}\Bigg)d\tau

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\|v(\tau,\cdot)-v_{\kappa}(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|v(\tau,\cdot)-v_{\kappa}(\tau,\cdot)\|_{\dot{B}^{1}_{\infty,\infty}}^{\frac{1}{2}}d\tau.

Hence, the first point
(1)
, together with the fact that

\|v(\tau,\cdot)-v_{\kappa}(\tau,\cdot)\|_{\dot{B}^{1}_{\infty,\infty}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\leqslant\|\omega(\tau,\cdot)-\omega_{\kappa}(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\leqslant C_{0,T}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

imply that

\displaystyle\|\Psi(t,\cdot)-\Psi_{\kappa}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle C_{0,T}\int_{0}^{t}\|\Psi(\tau,\cdot)-\Psi_{\kappa}(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}}\log\Bigg(\frac{e}{\|\Psi(\tau,\cdot)-\Psi_{\kappa}(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}}}\Bigg)d\tau

\displaystyle+tC_{0,T}\Big(\|v_{0,\kappa}-v_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\rho_{0,\kappa}-\rho_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\kappa T\Big)^{\frac{1}{4}\exp(-TC_{0,T})}.

Consequently, by using Osgood’s Lemma
A.1
, we find that

\|\Psi_{\kappa}(t,\cdot)-\Psi(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leqslant C_{0,T}\Big(\|v_{0,\kappa}-v_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\rho_{0,\kappa}-\rho_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\kappa t\Big)^{\frac{1}{4}\exp(-TC_{0,T})e^{-t}}.

Thus the second result follows by changing the value of
\widetilde{C}_{0,T}.

(3)
We turn now to prove the last convergence result. We write first

\displaystyle\|\gamma_{t,\kappa}(\cdot)-\gamma_{t}(\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\Psi_{\kappa}(t,\gamma^{0}(\cdot))-\Psi(t,\gamma^{0}(\cdot))\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\|\Psi_{\kappa}(t,\cdot)-\Psi(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}}

Thereafter,
in view of the estimate
(2)

we infer that, for all
t\in[0,T]

\|\gamma_{t,\kappa}(\cdot)-\gamma_{t}(\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim C_{0,T}\Big(\|v_{0,\kappa}-v_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\rho_{0,\kappa}-\rho_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\kappa t\Big)^{\frac{1}{4}\exp(-TC_{0,T})}

(5.6)

which yields the convergence in
L^{\infty}.
On the other hand, owing to Theorem
1.1
and Theorem
1.2
, we get

\|\gamma_{t,\kappa}(\cdot)-\gamma_{t}(\cdot)\|_{C^{s^{\prime}}}\leqslant C_{0,T},\quad\forall s^{\prime}<s.

Consequently, by interpolation we find that for all
\bar{s}\in(0,s^{\prime})

\displaystyle\|\gamma_{t,\kappa}(\cdot)-\gamma_{t}(\cdot)\|_{C^{\bar{s}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\gamma_{t,\kappa}(\cdot)-\gamma_{t}(\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{s^{\prime}-\bar{s}}{s^{\prime}}}\|\gamma_{t,\kappa}(\cdot)-\gamma_{t}(\cdot)\|_{C^{s^{\prime}}}^{\frac{\bar{s}}{s^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\|\gamma_{t,\kappa}(\cdot)-\gamma_{t}(\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{s^{\prime}-\bar{s}}{s^{\prime}}}C_{0,T}.

Therefore, (
5.6
) yields the convergence in
C^{\bar{s}}(\mathbb{R})
for all
\bar{s}<s
. Theorem
5.1
is then proved.
∎

We emphasize that the following variation of Theorem
5.1
holds. The proof is straightforward and uses the same arguments of the proof of Theorem
5.1
.

Corollary 5.1
.

Let
(n,\kappa)\in\mathbb{N}\times[0,1]
and
(v_{n,\kappa},\rho_{n,\kappa})
be a local solution of (
Bκ
) with initial data
(v_{n}^{0},\rho_{n}^{0})\in(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))^{2}
. Assume that the sequence of solutions
(v_{n,\kappa},\rho_{n,\kappa})_{(n,\kappa)\in\mathbb{N}\times[0,1]}
is defined on some common interval of time
[0,T]
and we suppose that
\omega_{n,\kappa}=\text{rot }v_{n,\kappa}
satisfies, for all
(n,\kappa)\in\mathbb{N}\times[0,1]

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N},\kappa\in[0,1]}\left(\big\|\big(v_{\kappa}(t),v(t)\big)\big\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\omega_{n,\kappa}(t)\|_{L^{2}\cap L^{\infty}}+\|\rho_{n,\kappa}(t)\|_{W^{1,2}\cap W^{1,\infty}}\right)\leqslant C_{0,T},\quad\forall t\in[0,T],

for some constant
C_{0,T}
. Then, the following holds for any
t\in[0,T]
and for any
(n,m,\kappa)\in\mathbb{N}^{2}\times[0,1]

\|v_{n,\kappa}(t)-v_{m,\kappa}(t)\|_{L^{2}}+\|\rho_{n,\kappa}(t)-\rho_{m,\kappa}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\leqslant C_{0,T}\left(\|v_{n}^{0}-v^{0}_{m}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\rho_{n}^{0}-\rho^{0}_{m}\|_{L^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}\exp(-TC_{0,T})}

as long as

C_{0,T}\Big(\|v_{0,\kappa}-v_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\rho_{0,\kappa}-\rho_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}\exp(-TC_{0,T})}\lesssim 1.

Remark 5.2
.

Corollary
5.1
states that the solutions to both systems (
B0
) and (
Bκ
) are
L^{2}
-stable provided that they are regular enough and satisfy good estimates. This was used before in step 3 in the proof of Theorem
3.1
concerning the
L^{2}
-stability of scheme (
3.38
).

Appendix A
Useful lemmas

We shall collect some classical results used throughout this paper. We start with recalling the following version of Osgood lemma which is a particular case of
[
4
, Lemma 3.4]
.

Lemma A.1
.

Let
f
be a measurable function from
[t_{0},T]
to
[0,a]
,
\gamma
a locally integrable function from
[t_{0},T]
to
\mathbb{R}^{+}
. Assume that, for some non-negative real number
c
, the function
f
satisfies

f(t)\leqslant c+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\gamma(\tau)f(\tau)\log\Bigg(\frac{a}{f(\tau)}\Bigg)d\tau,\quad for\text{ a.e. }t\in[t_{0},T].

Then, we have, for a.e.
t\in[t_{0},T]

f(t)\leqslant c^{\exp\left(-\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\gamma(\tau)d\tau\right)}a^{1-\exp\left(-\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\gamma(\tau)d\tau\right)}.

We recall now a technical estimate due to M. Vishik in
[
69
]
, which is very important in proof of the smoothing effect in Proposition
2.5
.

Lemma A.2
.

Let
d\geqslant 2
. There exists a positive constant depends only of the dimension
d
be such that, for every function
f
in Shwartz’s class and for every diffeomorphism
\Psi
of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
preserving Lebesgue’s measure, we have for all
(p,l)\in[1,\infty]\times\mathbb{N}
and for all
j,q\geqslant-1

\|\Delta_{j}(\Delta_{q}f\circ\Psi(\cdot))\|_{L^{p}}\leqslant C^{l}2^{-l|j-q|}\|\nabla^{l}\Psi^{\varepsilon(j,q)}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\Delta_{q}f\|_{L^{p}},

with

\varepsilon(j,q)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\textnormal{sign}(j-q)&\text{if }j\neq q,\\
0&\text{if }j=q.\end{array}\right.

Next, we recall the following commutator estimate from
[
41
]
.

Lemma A.3
.

Let
v
be a vector field of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
in free-divergence and belongs to
LL
. Let a given
f\in L^{p}
, with
p\in[1,\infty]
, and we define the commutator
{\bf R}_{q}

{\bf R}_{q}\triangleq S_{q-1}v\cdot\nabla f_{q}-\Delta_{q}(v\cdot\nabla f),\quad\forall q\geqslant-1.

Then, there exists an absolute constant
C>0
be such that, for every
q\geqslant-1

and
s\in(-1,1)
, we have

\|{\bf R}_{q}\|_{L^{p}}\leqslant C\bigg(\frac{1}{1-s}+\frac{1}{(1+s)^{2}}\bigg)\|v\|_{LL}(q+2)2^{-qs}\|f\|_{B^{s}_{p,\infty}}.

Now, we provide a self-contained proof of the De Giorgi-Nash estimates in any dimension
d\geqslant 2
. The proof below is based on suitable modification of the proof from
[
44
]
in dimension three.

Lemma A.4
.

Let
d\geqslant 2
,
(p,\,q,\,p_{1},\,q_{1})\in[1,\infty]^{4}
and
r\in[2,\infty]
with

{2\over p}+{d\over q}<1,\quad{2\over p_{1}}+{d\over q_{1}}<2.

Consider the equation

\partial_{t}f+u\cdot\nabla f-\kappa\Delta f=\nabla\cdot F+G,\quad t>0,\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\quad f(0,x)=f_{0}(x).

(A.1)

There exists
C>0
such that for every smooth divergence-free vector field
u
,
for every
F\in L^{p}_{T}L^{q}
and for every
f_{0}\in L^{r}
, the solution of (
A.1
) satisfies
the following estimate, for every
t>0

\displaystyle\|f(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle C\Big(1+{(\kappa t)^{-d\over 2r}}\Big){\|f_{0}\|_{L^{r}}}+C\Big(1+(\kappa t)^{\frac{1}{2}-\big({1\over p}+{d\over{2q}}\big)}\Big){\kappa^{\frac{1}{p}-1}\|F\|_{L^{p}_{t}L^{q}}}

(A.2)

\displaystyle+C\Big(1+(\kappa t)^{1-\big({1\over p_{1}}+{d\over 2q_{1}}\big)}\Big){\kappa^{\frac{1}{p_{1}}-1}\|G\|_{L^{p_{1}}_{t}L^{q_{1}}}}.

Proof.

Since the equation is linear, the problem can equivalently be reduced to the study of the following three systems

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\mathcal{P}_{\kappa}f=\nabla\cdot F,\\
f(0,x)=0.\end{array}\right.\quad\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\mathcal{P}_{\kappa}f=G,\\
f(0,x)=0.\end{array}\right.\quad\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\mathcal{P}_{\kappa}f=0,\\
f(0,x)=f_{0}(x),\end{array}\right.

(A.3)

where,
\mathcal{P}_{\kappa}f\triangleq\left(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla-\kappa\Delta\right)f.

In fact, we treat the case
\kappa=1
, then by a rescaling argument we obtain the desired estimate for the general case
\kappa>0
. Let us denote
\mathcal{P}\triangleq\mathcal{P}_{1}
and we begin with the first problem in (
A.3
).

\bullet

Step 1
:
T=1
and
\|F\|_{L^{p}_{1}L^{q}}\leqslant 1
.
Thanks to the divergence-free condition of
u
, a standard
L^{q}
-energy estimate gives

\frac{d}{dt}{1\over q}\|f(t)\|_{L^{q}}^{q}+\left(q-1\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|\nabla f\right|^{2}\left|f\right|^{q-2}dx\leqslant(q-1)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|F\right|\left|\nabla f\right|\left|f\right|^{q-2}dx.

On the other hand, we write

\left|F\right|\left|\nabla f\right|\left|f\right|^{q-2}=\left|F\right|\left|\nabla f\right|\left|f\right|^{\frac{q-2}{2}}\left|f\right|^{\frac{q-2}{2}}.

By assumption
q>d\geqslant 2
, Young and Hölder inequalities, we infer that

\frac{d}{dt}{1\over q}\|f(t)\|_{L^{q}}^{q}\leqslant(q-1)\|F\|_{L^{q}}^{2}\|f\|_{L^{q}}^{q-2}.

By using the fact that
p>2
and
\|F\|_{L^{p}_{1}L^{q}}\leqslant 1
, then by an integration in time, we get

\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{1}L^{q}}\leqslant C_{q}\left(\int_{0}^{1}\|F(t)\|_{L^{q}}^{2}dt\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leqslant C_{q}.

(A.4)

The improvement of the above estimate to reach the
L^{\infty}
norm in space requires exploring the iteration argument following De Giorgi-Nash. For this purpose, let us introduce the parameter
M>0
to be chosen later, and let
(M_{k})_{k\geqslant 0}
be a positive increasing sequence converging to
M

M_{k}\triangleq M\left(1-\frac{1}{k+1}\right).

An elementary computation gives then

\frac{d}{dt}\left(\frac{1}{2}\|(f-M_{k})_{+}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+\|\nabla(f-M_{k})_{+}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leqslant\left(\int_{f\geqslant M_{k}}|F|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla(f-M_{k})_{+}\|_{L^{2}},

with
x_{+}\triangleq\max(x,0)
. By denoting

U_{k}\triangleq\|(f-M_{k})_{+}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{1}L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla(f-M_{k})_{+}\|_{L^{2}_{1}L^{2}}^{2},

we obtain

U_{k}\leqslant\int_{0}^{1}\int_{f\geqslant M_{k}}|F|^{2}dxdt.

(A.5)

We claim an estimate of the form

U_{k}\leqslant C(M,k)U_{k}^{\gamma},

(A.6)

for some constant
C(M,k)
depending on
M
and
K
, and some
\gamma>1.
To do so, we introduce the positive quantity

m_{k}(t)\triangleq\left|\left\{x,f(t)\geqslant M_{k}\right\}\right|

and we notice that if
f(t,x)\geqslant M_{k}
then

f(t,x)-M_{k-1}\geqslant M_{k}-M_{k-1}\geqslant 0

(A.7)

and

\mathbf{1}_{f(t,x)-M_{k}}\leqslant\frac{(k+1)^{2}}{M}\left(f(t,x)-M_{k-1}\right)_{+}.

This yields

m_{k}(t)\leqslant\frac{(k+1)^{2m}}{M^{m}}\|\left(f(t,x)-M_{k-1}\right)_{+}\|_{L^{m}}^{m},

(A.8)

for some
m\geqslant 1
to be chosen later. Now, we resume from (
A.5
) and we use Hölder inequality to obtain

U_{k}\leqslant\int_{0}^{1}\|F(t)\|_{L^{q}}^{2}m_{k}(t)^{1-\frac{2}{q}}dt\leqslant\|F\|_{L^{p}_{1}L^{q}}^{2}\left(\int_{0}^{1}m_{k}(t)^{(1-\frac{2}{q})(\frac{p}{p-2})}dt\right)^{1-\frac{2}{p}}.

(A.9)

Implementing (
A.8
) yields to

U_{k}\leqslant\|F\|_{L^{p}_{1}L^{q}}^{2}\left(\frac{(k+1)^{2}}{M}\right)^{m(1-\frac{2}{p})}\left(\int_{0}^{1}\|\left(f(t,x)-M_{k-1}\right)_{+}\|_{L^{m}}^{m(1-\frac{2}{q})(\frac{p}{p-2})}dt\right)^{1-\frac{2}{p}}.

Let
d^{\star}
be given by

d^{\star}\triangleq\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\infty&\text{if }d=2,\\
\frac{2d}{d-2},&\text{ if }d\geqslant 3.\end{array}\right.

By interpolation, one gets

\|\left(f(t)-M_{k-1}\right)_{+}\|_{L^{\alpha}_{1}L^{\beta}}^{2}\leqslant U_{k-1},

(A.10)

for all
4
4

4

Notice that when
d\geqslant 3
,
\beta
is allowed to take the value
d^{\star}
in (
A.10
).

\alpha\geqslant 2
,
\beta\in[2,d^{\star})
and
\frac{2}{\alpha}+\frac{d}{\beta}\geqslant\frac{d}{2}.

In order to achieve our claim (
A.6
), we need to choose
m
in
[2,d^{\star})
such that

m\left(1-\frac{2}{q}\right)>2,\quad\quad 2\frac{1-\frac{2}{p}}{m\left(1-\frac{2}{q}\right)}+\frac{d}{m}\geqslant\frac{d}{2}.

(A.11)

The first condition is satisfied whenever

\frac{2}{1-\frac{2}{q}}<d^{\star}.

This is equivalent to say that
q>\frac{2d^{\star}}{d^{\star}-2}=d
, which is automatically satisfied due to the hypothesis
\frac{2}{p}+\frac{d}{q}<1.

For the second condition in (
A.11
), it is satisfied if

2\left(1-\frac{2}{p}\right)+d\left(1-\frac{2}{q}\right)\geqslant m\left(1-\frac{2}{q}\right)\frac{d}{2}\geqslant 2\cdot\frac{d}{2}=d,

which is equivalent to the assumption
\frac{2}{p}+\frac{d}{q}<1.
Hence, as a consequence we obtain (
A.6
). More precisely, we find

U_{k}\leqslant\left(\frac{(k+1)^{2}}{M}\right)^{m(1-\frac{2}{p})}U_{k-1}^{\gamma},\quad\forall k\geqslant 1.

In particular, if
U_{0}
is sufficiently small, we obtain
\displaystyle\lim_{k\rightarrow+\infty}U_{k}=0
. Thus, Fatou Lemma yields for all
t\in[0,1]

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(f(t,x)-M\right)_{+}dx\leqslant 0,

and therefore, for almost
(t,x)\in[0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}

f(t,x)\leqslant M.

By changing
f
into
-f
, similar arguments lead to

f(t,x)\geqslant-M,

and finally we obtain

\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{1}L^{\infty}}\leqslant M.

Let us recall that the above scenario is true provided that
U_{0}
is sufficiently small, and this can happen by choosing
M
large enough in the following inequality which comes from (
A.9
), the Tchebychev inequality and the energy inequality (
A.4
)

U_{0}\leqslant\left(\int_{0}^{1}m_{0}(t)^{(1-\frac{2}{q})(\frac{p}{p-2})}\right)^{1-\frac{2}{p}}\leqslant\left(\frac{\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{1}L^{q}}}{M-1}\right)^{q-2}\leqslant\left(\frac{C_{q}}{M-1}\right)^{q-2}.

This ends the estimates for the first problem in (
A.3
) in the case
T\leqslant 1
and
\|F\|_{L^{p}_{1}L^{q}}\leqslant 1.

\bullet

Step 2
:
T\geqslant 1
and
\|F\|_{L^{p}_{1}L^{q}}\geqslant 1
.
In this case, we just need to follow closely
[
44
]
and make use of the following rescaling argument

\widetilde{f}(\tau,X)\triangleq\frac{1}{K}f(T\tau,{T}^{\frac{1}{2}}X),

\widetilde{F}(\tau,X)\triangleq\frac{{T}^{\frac{1}{2}}}{K}F(T\tau,{T}^{\frac{1}{2}}X),

K\triangleq{T}^{\frac{1}{2}-(\frac{1}{p}+\frac{d}{2q})}.

Under the previous rescaling,
\widetilde{f}
satisfies the equation

\partial_{\tau}\widetilde{f}+\widetilde{u}\cdot\nabla_{X}\widetilde{f}-\Delta_{X}=\nabla_{X}\cdot\widetilde{F},

and

\|\widetilde{F}\|_{L^{p}_{1}L^{q}}=1.

Thanks to the result of step 1, we derive

\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}}=K\|\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{1}L^{\infty}}\leqslant MK=M{T}^{\frac{1}{2}-\big(\frac{1}{p}+\frac{d}{2q}\big)}\|F\|_{L^{p}_{T}L^{q}}.

Let us now outline the proof of the estimate for the second problem in (
A.3
). We only give some details of the proof in the case
q_{1}<\infty
, while
q_{1}=\infty
is a direct consequence of the Maximum principle. For
G
in
L^{p_{1}}_{T}L^{q_{1}}
, we can write
G=\nabla\cdot F
, for some
F\in L^{p_{1}}_{T}W^{1,q_{1}}\hookrightarrow L^{p_{1}}L^{q_{1}^{\star}}
, where
q^{\star}=\frac{dq_{1}}{d-q_{1}}
and

\|F\|_{L^{p_{1}}_{T}L^{q^{*}}}\lesssim\|G\|_{L^{p_{1}}_{T}L^{q_{1}}}.

Note that
\frac{2}{p_{1}}+\frac{d}{q_{1}^{\star}}<1
is equivalent to
\frac{2}{p_{1}}+\frac{d}{q_{1}}<2
. Hence, the estimate already proved for the first system in (
A.3
) achieves the proof of the desired estimate for the second system.

Finally, the estimates of the last system in (
A.3
) can be done along the same lines as in
[
44
]
. Indeed, the proof presented in
[
44
]
is done in two steps: the first step consists in proving

\sup_{t\geqslant 1}\|f(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leqslant M.

This step does not exploit any particular properties related to the spacial variables, hence, it can be easily extended to any dimension
d\geqslant 2
. Meanwhile, the second step consists in treating the case
t\leqslant 1
. We emphasize that the idea uses the same arguments explored in our treatment of the first system in (
A.3
). This justifies the apparition of the term
t^{-\frac{d}{2r}}
. The details of this are left to the reader. Lemma (
A.4
) is then proved.
∎
